    	<span><i class="fa fa-tasks"></i> <a href="javascript:void(0);" title="Components:" class="contains-assets" data-html="true" data-content="&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;Regenerative Medicine in the World of Lasers Recording&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;Lasers in Aesthetic Use - Steven Bengelsdorf, MD - Handout&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;CME SESSION EVALUATION&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;Certification/Credit&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;" data-placement="top">Contains 4 Component(s)</a>, <a class="contains-assets credits-details" href="javascript:void(0);" title="Available Credits:" data-placement="top" data-html="true" data-content="&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;Certification/Credit: 1.50 to 1.70 CME for 3 types available&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;">Includes Credits</a></span>

        <div class="summaryCollapse">
                            <p>This advanced course explores how regenerative medicine is reshaping laser and energy-based device outcomes by optimizing the skin's biological microenvironment. Faculty will review the latest science behind regenerative healing, highlight ideal biologic sources for enhanced repair, and discuss evidence-based strategies to prep and prime patients before and after laser treatments. Through practical algorithms, preclinical evidence, and case-based insights, attendees will learn how to integrate energy-based devices with regenerative elements--exosomes, growth factors, platelet-derived therapies, peptides, and more--to accelerate recovery, improve durability, and achieve best-in-class aesthetic results. Participants will leave with a clear framework for combining lasers and regenerative options safely, effectively, and ethically in clinical practice.</p>
                                </div>
    

            
        <div class="tabCollapse" id="collapse_1341396_255282">
                                    <p><strong><em>The course was recorded on May 7, 2026, at the ASLMS Annual Conference.</em></strong></p>
<p>Launch Date: 6/10/26</p>
<p>Expiration Date: 11/30/26</p>
<p><strong>Course Description: </strong>This advanced course explores how regenerative medicine is reshaping laser and energy-based device outcomes by optimizing the skin’s biological microenvironment. Faculty will review the latest science behind regenerative healing, highlight ideal biologic sources for enhanced repair, and discuss evidence-based strategies to prep and prime patients before and after laser treatments. Through practical algorithms, preclinical evidence, and case-based insights, attendees will learn how to integrate energy-based devices with regenerative elements—exosomes, growth factors, platelet-derived therapies, peptides, and more—to accelerate recovery, improve durability, and achieve best-in-class aesthetic results. Participants will leave with a clear framework for combining lasers and regenerative options safely, effectively, and ethically in clinical practice.
  </p>
<p><strong>COURSE DIRECTORS: </strong>Diane Duncan, MD, FACS - Plastic Surgical Associates; Saranya P. Wyles, MD, PhD – Director, Regenerative Dermatology and Skin Longevity Laboratory, Department of Dermatology, Mayo Clinic, Rochester, MN
  </p>
<p><strong>FACULTY PANELISTS:</strong> Diane Duncan, MD, FACS; Saranya P. Wyles, MD, PhD; Steven Bengelsdorf, MD; Greg Chernoff, MD; Jill S. Waibel, MD; Melinda Lacerna Kimbrell, MD, FACS;
  </p>
<p><strong>LEARNING OBJECTIVES:</strong>
  </p>
<p>At the end of the activity, learners should be able to:
  </p>
<ol>  <li>Objective      1: Understanding that lasers and regeneratives are not an either/or, but a      synergistic blend of energy priming followed by specific regenerative      elements that can modulate wound healing, scarring, and tissue      regeneration.               </li><li>Practical      use: protocols and guidance for use in shortening post-resurfacing      recovery, scar revision, hair restoration and skin rejuvenation.&nbsp;
 </li></ol>
<p>&nbsp;<strong>TARGET AUDIENCE:</strong></p>
<p>This activity is intended for an audience of physicians and other healthcare professionals interested in the use of lasers, light, energy-based devices, and related technologies across various specialties.
  </p>
<p><strong>PRESENTATIONS:</strong>
  </p>
<p>Rethinking the Role of Lasers in Aesthetic Medicine | Steven Bengelsdorf, MD – Medical Director, Franklin Skin and Laser   </p>
<p>Combining EBDs with Regenerative Elements | Greg Chernoff, MD – Chernoff Plastic Surgery
  </p>
<p>Biological Enablers: Regenerative Therapies with Laser and Laser-Assisted Drug Delivery | Jill S. Waibel, MD – Miami Dermatology &amp; Laser Institute, Miami, FL
  </p>
<p>Plasma Skin Resurfacing and Exosomes: Perfect Together | Melinda Lacerna Kimbrell, MD, FACS – Plastic Surgeon, LA Plastic Surgery
  </p>
<p>How to Combine Lasers and Regenerative Options in Your Practice for Best Outcomes | Diane Duncan, MD, FACS - Plastic Surgical Associates
  </p>
<p>Energy Meets Longevity: Lasers as Catalysts for Regenerative Skin Health | Saranya P. Wyles, MD, PhD – Director, Regenerative Dermatology and Skin Longevity Laboratory, Department of Dermatology, Mayo Clinic, Rochester, MN   </p>
<p><strong>DISCLOSURES:</strong>
  </p>
<p>The American Society for Laser Medicine and Surgery (ASLMS) adheres to the policies and guidelines, including the <em>Standards for Integrity and Independence in Accredited CE</em>, set forth to providers by the Accreditation Council for Continuing Medical Education (ACCME), stating those activities where continuing education credits are awarded must be balanced, independent, objective, and scientifically rigorous. All persons in a position to control the content of an accredited continuing education program provided by ASLMS are required to disclose all financial relationships with any ineligible company within the past 24 months.&nbsp; All financial relationships reported are identified as relevant and mitigated by ASLMS in accordance with the <em>Standards for Integrity and Independence in Accredited CE</em> in advance of delivery of the activity to learners. The content of this activity was vetted by ASLMS to assure objectivity and that the activity is free of commercial bias. All relevant financial relationships have been mitigated by ASLMS.
  </p>

  </p>

He completed his surgical training at the Boston University Surgical Residency Program, where he gained experience with fiber optic and energy-based devices in the operating room. With a strong interest in achieving surgical outcomes with less morbidity, he pursued advanced training in minimally invasive surgery at Beth Israel Hospital in New York City, providing the foundation for his lifelong commitment to advancing laser and light-based technologies in patient care.</p><p>Dr. Bengelsdorf went on to serve as a civilian contractor at Fort Campbell, Kentucky, home of the 101st Airborne Division, where he provided surgical expertise to support active military members and their families at Blanchfield Army Community Hospital. In 2005, he founded Franklin Skin and Laser, which has grown over the past 20 years into the leading physician-managed laser center in Williamson County, Tennessee. As Medical Director, he has developed expertise in advanced laser applications, including skin resurfacing, treatment of vascular lesions, tattoo removal, and comprehensive aesthetic medicine. He has been sought out for his opinions by multiple companies, including Allergan, Candela, and Acclaro Medical, often using his resources to test new devices and protocols.</p><p>Beyond his clinical practice, Dr. Bengelsdorf is committed to scholarship and education. He served as the medical reviewer for the textbook Lasers and Light Therapy and was invited faculty at the International Master Course on Aging Science (IMCAS) in Paris. He also contributes regularly to the IMCAS website, sharing collegial insights that reflect his global engagement in advancing aesthetic medicine. Additionally, he sponsors an internship program for undergraduate pre-medical students, providing hands-on exposure to clinical practice, research, and medical technology, including lasers and aesthetic medicine.</p><p>He has shared his insights through national television appearances, including âThe Doctors,â and in major publications such as The Wall Street Journal, Wired Magazine, and the Nashville Business Journal.</p><p>A Fellow of the American College of Surgeons, Dr. Bengelsdorf serves as a Physician Delegate to the Tennessee Medical Association and has been an active member of ASLMS for nearly 20 years. His candidacy for the ASLMS Board reflects his dedication to advancing the Societyâs mission by promoting research, education, and innovation in the safe and effective use of lasers and energy-based devices. He looks forward to bringing his blend of surgical expertise, innovation, and mentorship to help further advance the mission of ASLMS.</p></div>

Dr. Waibel lectures, collaborates, and trains military physicians to help the wounded warriors. Dr. Waibel makes her home in Coral Gables with her husband and four children.</p></div>

        <div class="summaryCollapse">
                            <p>Recorded at the 45th ASLMS Annual Conference on 5/7/2026 8AM - 9:30AM.</p>
                                </div>
    

            
        <div class="tabCollapse" id="collapse_1341401_255282">
                                    <p><strong><em>The course was recorded on May 7, 2026, at the ASLMS Annual Conference.</em></strong></p>
<p>Launch Date: 6/10/26</p>
<p>Expiration Date: 11/30/26</p>
<p><strong></strong><strong>Course Description: </strong>This course will examine the fundamental aspects of laser tissue interactions. We will begin with basic concepts such as skin optics and the subsequent migration of light energy into the skin, the absorption of laser energy into the skin, and creation of heat.</p>
<p>Then the field will be broken down into ablative and not ablative technologies, and finally visible light and near infrared technologies for pigmented and vascular lesions.
  </p>
<p>The attendee should expect to, at the end of the course, have an appreciation for basic physical principles that guide laser procedures in cutaneous applications.
  </p>
<p><strong>COURSE DIRECTORS: </strong>E. Victor Ross, MD – Director of Laser and Cosmetic Dermatology, Scripps Clinic; Kelly Stankiewicz, MD, FAAD - Dermatologist / Owner, The Dermatology House, Park City, UT
  </p>
<p><strong>FACULTY PANELISTS:</strong> E. Victor Ross, MD; Nicole M. Golbari, MD, MPH; Morgan B. Murphrey, MD; Farah A. Moustafa, MD; Kelly Stankiewicz, MD, FAAD   </p>
<p><strong>LEARNING OBJECTIVES:</strong>
  </p>
<p>At the end of the activity, learners should be able to:
  </p>
<ol>  <li>Describe      how to apply the principles of laser-tissue interactions.
  </li><li>Explain      how to choose the appropriate technology for the treatment of different      medical conditions.
  </li><li>Identify      laser therapeutic and warning endpoints.
  </li><li>Summarize      best practices for using lasers.
 </li></ol>
<p><strong>TARGET AUDIENCE:</strong>
  </p>
<p>This activity is intended for an audience of physicians and other healthcare professionals interested in the use of lasers, light, energy-based devices, and related technologies across various specialties.
  </p>
<p><strong>PRESENTATIONS:</strong>
  </p>
<p>Intro &amp; Laser Basics: Inside the Devices | E. Victor Ross, MD – Director of Laser and Cosmetic Dermatology, Scripps Clinic   </p>
<p>Ablative and Non-Ablative Resurfacing | Nicole M. Golbari, MD, MPH – NYU Grossman Long Island School of Medicine, Division of Dermatology
  </p>
<p>Vascular Lasers | Morgan B. Murphrey, MD – Dermatologist, Dermatology Partners, Inc.
  </p>
<p>Pigment and Tattoo Lasers | Farah A. Moustafa, MD – Assistant Professor, Director Laser &amp; Cosmetics Center, Tufts Medical Center
  </p>
<p>Practice Pearls &amp; Endpoints | Kelly Stankiewicz, MD, FAAD – Dermatologist / Owner, The Dermatology House, Park City, UT
  </p>
<p><strong>DISCLOSURES:</strong>
  </p>
<p>The American Society for Laser Medicine and Surgery (ASLMS) adheres to the policies and guidelines, including the <em>Standards for Integrity and Independence in Accredited CE</em>, set forth to providers by the Accreditation Council for Continuing Medical Education (ACCME), stating those activities where continuing education credits are awarded must be balanced, independent, objective, and scientifically rigorous. All persons in a position to control the content of an accredited continuing education program provided by ASLMS are required to disclose all financial relationships with any ineligible company within the past 24 months.&nbsp; All financial relationships reported are identified as relevant and mitigated by ASLMS in accordance with the <em>Standards for Integrity and Independence in Accredited CE</em> in advance of delivery of the activity to learners. The content of this activity was vetted by ASLMS to assure objectivity and that the activity is free of commercial bias. All relevant financial relationships have been mitigated by ASLMS.
  </p>

			<div class="speaker row-fluid">
            	<div class=" span2 "><img src="https://learn.aslms.org/files/9fba48e6-2256-4544-812b-423ac8b75a14" class="photo" title="Speaker image" alt="E. Victor Ross, MD"></div>
                <div class=" span10 ">
                    <h2 class="name">E. Victor Ross, MD (Moderator)</h2>
                    <h3 class="title"></h3>
                                        <div class="bio"><p>E. Victor Ross, M.D., CAPT MC, USN (ret) attended Mississippi State University where he graduated summa cum laude with a B.S. in physics. He obtained his medical degree from the Tulane University School of Medicine where he was a junior year inductee into Alpha Omega Alpha. Following internship, Dr. Ross went to Sasebo, Japan as a General Medical Officer. In 1992, he completed a three-year dermatology residency at the National Naval Medical Center in Bethesda. In 1994, he was the first Navy-sponsored fellow in photomedicine and lasers at the Massachusetts General Hospital. While there, he conducted basic research experiments and human clinical trials in photodynamic therapy and novel laser applications in dermatology. He was honored as the 2001 Navy Recipient of the Chairman, Joint Chiefs of Staff Award for excellence in Military Medicine.</p><p>Dr. Ross is presently the director of the Laser and Cosmetic Dermatology Center at Scripps Clinic in San Diego. He also served on the boards of the American Society for Laser Medicine and Surgery (president in 2008), the American Society for Dermatologic Surgery, and the American Society for Cosmetic Dermatology and Aesthetic Surgery. His special interests are fractional laser skin rejuvenation, photodynamic therapy, non-ablative skin remodeling, laser management of scars, laser treatment of acne, and laser hair reduction in dark skinned patients. He is the author of over 100 articles and nine book chapters. Presently, Dr. Ross is investigating novel approaches in pigment quantification as well as conducting comparative trials of picosecond laser sources, novel RF technologies in skin rejuvenation, photon recycling, and laser assisted acne treatments.</p></div>

                    <h2 class="name">Morgan Murphrey, MD</h2>
                    <h3 class="title"></h3>
                    						<h3 class="company">Wellman Center for Photomedicine, Harvard Medical School</h3>
					                    <div class="bio"><p>Morgan Murphrey is a board certified dermatologist, and dermatology fellow in Laser &amp; Cosmetic Dermatology at Massachusetts General Hospital, Harvard Department of Dermatology in Boston, MA. She completed her dermatology residency at the University of California, Davis, where she served as the chief resident in her final year of training. Before her residency, she attended Creighton University School of Medicine, where she concurrently earned her medical degree and Master's degree in Health Care Ethics. Between her third and fourth year of medical school, she solidified her passion for research and innovation while completing a pre-doctoral research fellowship at Northwestern University, where she participated in both industry and investigator-initiated clinical trials. She grew up in Northern California, and completed her undergraduate education at California Polytechnic State University in San Luis Obispo, where she earned a degree in Biochemistry and a minor in Psychology and French. She is passionate about finding innovative solutions to problems in dermatology.</p></div>
